Background (from the session that started this one):
Sejal Borkar is a product designer (GenAI product design intern at Epifi until the company winds down in Nov 2026, open to roles from Dec 2026). Her new pixel-art portfolio is live at sejalborkar.vercel.app. She runs several linked Claude chats: a main Figma chat for the portfolio design, a code chat that deploys the portfolio, and a cat chat for its mascot. This new chat is separate and only about job-hunt automation. She wants it to ask her questions first; she will then give prompts and answers. She prefers concise replies, wants pushback when something is risky, and wants Remote Control kept on for every chat.

Task:
This chat is "Automation scripts". The goal is to write scripts that speed up Sejal's job hunt:
1. Auto-apply on job sites like Instahyre and similar boards.
2. Watch LinkedIn for fresh openings (just posted, under 100 applicants, posts announcing a role) and apply or alert fast, before the crowd.

THIS FIRST TURN: do not build anything yet. Introduce yourself in two lines, then ask her the questions you need, grouped and numbered, short, with your recommended default next to each one so she can just say "ok". Cover at least:
- Target roles, seniority, locations or remote, and the companies or industries to prefer or avoid.
- Which sites, and what "apply" means on each (one-click, form fill, recruiter message, email).
- Filters for "fresh" (posted within X hours, under N applicants, keywords in posts).
- Resume and cover-note versions, plus the portfolio link to use (sejalborkar.vercel.app).
- Fully automatic or human-in-the-loop (her one-tap approve before each submit), and how she wants alerts (email, phone, Slack, desktop).
- Where it runs (her Mac on a schedule, or a cloud runner) and how often.
- Budget: default is free tools only.
- Where results are logged (a sheet or a local file).
Then wait for her answers and prompts.

BE HER SENIOR ADVISOR, NOT JUST AN ASSISTANT. Before she commits, say plainly in a few lines:
- LinkedIn's User Agreement forbids scraping and automated actions, and accounts that do it get restricted or banned. Some job boards have similar terms.
- Recommend the lower-risk route: official job alerts, email and RSS parsing, and saved searches, with fast human-approved applies, rate limits, and her own logged-in browser session.
- Let her decide.

Hard limits:
- Never bypass or solve CAPTCHAs or other bot detection.
- Never hard-code or store her passwords in code or files. She logs in herself; scripts reuse her own browser session or profile.
- Never send messages or submit applications on her behalf without the flow she approves.
- Never spend money or paid credits without asking.

Working rules:
- Plain, short English.
- No em dashes in any copy you write for her (cover notes, messages, docs).
- Keep a single README.md in this folder as the tracker: what exists, how to run it, what is done and what is left. Don't make extra tracker files.
- Use git locally once code exists. Create a GitHub repo (private, under SBorkar2002) only if she asks.

Shared notes folder for this group of sessions (outside the repo; read siblings' notes there, leave hand-over notes or large findings there instead of pasting them into messages): /Users/sejal/Library/Application Support/Claude/side-session-notes/local_633c2f75-4daa-4320-bffb-4a1f299ead17

## ASSISTANT
Hi Sejal, I'm your job-hunt automation partner for this folder.
I'll ask questions first and only build once you've answered. I'll also push back on anything that could cost you an account or a recruiter's goodwill.

**First, the risk**
- LinkedIn's User Agreement bans scraping, bots and automated actions, including extensions that click for you. Accounts caught doing this get restricted, sometimes for good. LinkedIn is your main design network, so losing it mid-hunt is the worst thing that could happen here.
- Some other boards have similar rules. I'll read each site's terms before building anything for it.
- Applying to lots of jobs automatically can also hurt designers. Recruiters notice poor-fit applications and generic notes. Your portfolio wins the job; speed only helps on top of it.

**What I recommend (lower risk, still fast)**
- **LinkedIn:** no script ever logs in or clicks there. You turn on LinkedIn's own job alerts set to "as soon as posted." A script reads those alert emails in your Gmail, filters them and pings your phone within minutes. You apply yourself, with a short note and your standard answers ready to paste.
- **Company career pages:** many startups post first on hiring tools like Greenhouse, Lever or Ashby. Those publish public job feeds that are meant to be read, so watching them is allowed, and they often show roles hours before LinkedIn does.
- **Instahyre and similar sites:** the script works in your own logged-in Chrome, about 15 applications a day at most, with gaps between them. You tap approve before each one is sent.
- **Hiring posts:** turn on the bell for key design leads and recruiters on LinkedIn. The script then watches those notification emails.

Your call: **A)** the route above, or **B)** also automate LinkedIn in your browser, accepting the ban risk. My limits apply either way: no getting past CAPTCHAs, no stored passwords, and nothing sent without your approval.

**Questions** (reply "ok to all", or only the numbers you want to change)

**1. Roles**
1.1 Job titles? Default: Product Designer, UX Designer, UI/UX Designer, AI / GenAI Product Designer, Interaction Designer.
1.2 Level? Default: entry level and Associate (0 to 2 years). Skip Senior, Lead and Principal.
1.3 Locations? Default: Bengaluru, Mumbai, or remote within India.
1.4 Start date? Default: Dec 2026. Roles that want "immediate joiners" get flagged, not skipped.
1.5 Prefer? Default: AI products, fintech, consumer apps and design-led startups.
1.6 Avoid? Default: agencies, IT services firms, unpaid or contract-only roles, and betting apps.
1.7 Dream companies? Default: you send 20 to 40 names. Their openings ping you instantly, even if they miss a filter.

**2. Sites and what "apply" means**
2.1 Which sites? Default: LinkedIn (alerts only), Instahyre, Wellfound, Cutshort, and company career pages. Do you want Naukri, Hirist or YC Work at a Startup too?
2.2 What can the script do? Default: one-click applies and short forms, filled in for you to approve. Messages to recruiters and cold emails are drafted only; you send them yourself.
2.3 Standard form answers (current and expected pay, notice period, relocation, phone)? Default: you give them once, and they're kept in a local file that never goes online.

**3. What counts as "fresh"**
3.1 How recent? Default: posted within 24 hours. Anything under 3 hours old gets an urgent ping.
3.2 How many applicants? Default: under 100. LinkedIn only shows this on the job page, so with route A you'll see it when you open the link.
3.3 Post keywords? Default: "hiring" plus "product designer", "UX designer" or "design role".
3.4 Words to exclude? Default: senior, lead, principal, manager, 5+ years, intern. Do you want internships too?

**4. Resume, cover note, portfolio**
4.1 Resume? Default: the PDF already on your site at /resume, as the only version for now.
4.2 Cover note? Default: one 3-line template I draft, filled in with the company, the role and one line that matches the job post. AI-written notes would cost money, so they're off. You edit before anything is sent.
4.3 Portfolio link? Default: sejalborkar.vercel.app with a small tag for each site (like ?ref=instahyre), so you can see which site sends visitors. That needs Vercel's free analytics turned on.
4.4 Email for applications? Default: your personal Gmail, not your Epifi one, since that account closes in November.

**5. Control and alerts**
5.1 Automatic or approve-first? Default: you approve every application. After a week we can loosen this for one site if it goes well.
5.2 How should alerts reach you? Default: a free Telegram bot that pushes to your phone with Approve and Skip buttons, plus Mac pop-ups and one email summary at 9pm. Slack only if it's a personal workspace, not Epifi's.

**6. Where it runs and how often**
6.1 Where? Default: on your Mac, using a separate Chrome profile that you log into yourself.
6.2 How often? Default: every 20 minutes from 8am to 11pm IST, quiet overnight.

**7. Budget**
7.1 Default: free tools only. I'll ask before anything paid.

**8. Where results are logged**
8.1 Default: a Google Sheet on your personal account so you can check it on your phone, plus a copy saved on your Mac. Columns: date, site, company, role, link, posted time, applicants, status, follow-up date.

**9. Build order**
9.1 Default: Gmail alerts and phone pings first, then company career pages, then Instahyre with approve-first, then the rest.

I'll keep `README.md` as the single tracker and save versions locally with git. I won't create a GitHub repo unless you ask.

## ASSISTANT
I wrote your seven points into [README.md](README.md), along with the sheet columns (posted time, applied time, minutes to apply, links, status, response date and more). Every answer you give from now on gets stored there, so it can't get lost.

**Point 4: where to start**
First the shared setup: the Google Sheet with its tabs, phone alerts, and a file of your standard answers. Then I suggest the **LinkedIn job-alert watcher** as the first source:
- It's the most jobs for the least risk, since it reads LinkedIn's own alert emails and never logs into LinkedIn.
- It can run in the cloud, so it works with your laptop off.
- It tells you within minutes of a job going up.

After that comes Instahyre, then Wellfound, then Cutshort.

**Point 5: is it code injected into the website?**
No. There are two kinds of script:
- **Watchers** find jobs. They read your email and public job feeds, and never touch the job site's page.
- **Appliers** open a real browser on its own Chrome profile that you log into yourself. They fill in the form and wait for your approval. To the site, it looks like you using Chrome.

**Point 6: running with the laptop off**
- **Watchers: yes, for free.** Google Apps Script runs inside your Google account on a timer. It can read your Gmail, write to the Sheet and ping your phone, so no Mac is needed.
- **Appliers: here is the risk.** To apply while your laptop is off, a cloud computer has to stay logged in as you, which means storing your saved login (not your password) online. Logins from cloud servers also look like bots, so Instahyre and LinkedIn are likely to show CAPTCHAs, which I won't get past, or to flag your account.
- **My suggestion:** watchers run 24/7 in the cloud. Applying happens with one tap from your phone, and that tap applies from your Mac when it's awake. Or you open the link and apply yourself.

**About "be the first applicant":** speed does help. But on LinkedIn, recruiters usually sort applicants by best match, not by who came first. So a fast, well-matched application beats a fast, generic one. The filters will stay strict.

**Questions** (questions 1 to 9 from my last message still stand; "ok" accepts the defaults)

10. Is the LinkedIn route A (alerts only, no automation) or B (also automate LinkedIn, ban risk accepted)? Default: A.
11. Which Google account should own the Sheet and read the job-alert emails? Default: your personal Gmail, not Epifi.
12. Should applying be "tap on phone, then your Mac applies" or "fully in the cloud, with your saved login stored online"? Default: tap on phone, Mac applies.
13. Telegram for phone alerts and the approve button? Default: yes, it's free.
14. Do you want the first source to be the LinkedIn alerts watcher, then Instahyre, Wellfound and Cutshort? Default: yes.
15. Private answers like expected pay, notice period and phone number: you'll type these into a file that stays on your Mac only. OK? Default: yes.

## What the setup is, in plain words

You can think of the system as a small office with three parts:

| Part | What it is | Why we need it |
|---|---|---|
| **Google Sheet** | Your job log | Every job found goes into a row, on the tab for its site. You can check it from your phone. |
| **Apps Script** | A small program that lives inside your Google account | This is the worker. Google runs it on a timer on their computers, so it keeps going with your laptop off, and it's free. It can write to your Sheet and read your Gmail without any extra logins. |
| **Telegram bot** | A chat in Telegram that only you and the script use | This is the doorbell. When a fresh job turns up, it sends a message to your phone straight away. Later it can show Approve and Skip buttons. Email is too slow, and an SMS service costs money. |
| **Token** | A secret key Telegram gives you for the bot | It proves to Telegram that the messages come from *your* script. It sits in a locked settings box inside Apps Script, never in the code, so it can't leak if the code is shared. |

The flow: **the Apps Script checks for jobs → logs them in the Sheet → pings your phone on Telegram → you apply.**

You only do the setup steps once. After that it runs on its own.

## LinkedIn every hour: yes, it's possible

Writing it is easy, and checking once an hour is fine. The problem isn't how often it runs. It's **whose account it runs as.**

1. **Logged in as you** (it scans and Easy Applies on your account): LinkedIn spots bots by how they behave, not just how often they visit. Even once an hour, automated Easy Apply on your account is what gets accounts restricted. I don't recommend this.
2. **Logged out** (it scans LinkedIn's public job search, "past hour" + newest first, with no account): your account is never touched, so it can't be banned. It's still against LinkedIn's rules. LinkedIn sometimes blocks these visits, and when that happens the script just gets nothing that hour. It can't apply, because applying needs your login. It **finds the job and pings you**, and you Easy Apply from your phone in about a minute.

**My recommendation: option 2, run hourly.** Each job reaches you within an hour of going up, your account stays safe, and you still get in early.

If you agree, I'll make that the first source after the setup. Sources:
- [ViralBrain: LinkedIn job alerts](https://www.viralbrain.ai/blog/linkedin-job-alerts)
- [Cooper Union: Setting job alerts on LinkedIn](https://cooper.edu/continuing-education/setting-job-alerts-linkedin)

## ASSISTANT
The workflow diagram is up: **[Job Hunt Workflow](https://claude.ai/artifact/9No5UMQKk65HViTicyM6SB)**. I haven't looked at the published page myself yet, so if the diagram comes out broken, tell me and I'll fix it.

It's a scrollable map of every step. Colors show where each step happens:
- **Blue: Google cloud.** Runs every hour, even with your laptop off. It finds jobs, filters them, skips ones already in the Sheet, logs the new ones and pings you.
- **Yellow: Telegram on your phone.**
- **Green: you.**
- **Purple: your Mac.** Only when it's awake, because that's where your logins live.

**Why Telegram, in one line:** it's the only way the system can reach you instantly *and* get a one-tap answer back. Without it you'd have to keep opening the Sheet to check, and nothing could wait for your approval.

**The 7 cases where Telegram is used:**
1. **New LinkedIn job:** a link to open. You apply yourself, then tap "I applied".
2. **New job on Instahyre, Cutshort or Wellfound:** Approve or Skip.
3. **Applied:** your Mac confirms it submitted.
4. **Needs you:** you got logged out, a CAPTCHA came up, or a form asked something it can't answer.
5. **Mac asleep:** approved jobs have been waiting over 3 hours.
6. **Follow-up:** the day to nudge a recruiter, with a message drafted for you.
7. **Daily summary** at 9pm.

What runs with your laptop off: finding jobs on all four sites. Applying on Instahyre, Cutshort and Wellfound needs your Mac awake, since your logins never go online. LinkedIn applying is always by hand.

One thing I haven't checked yet: how fast each site's job alert emails arrive. That decides how much of the finding for those three sites can run in the cloud.

The page is now a real flow diagram that moves as you scroll, and it's open on your screen. Same link: https://claude.ai/artifact/9No5UMQKk65HViTicyM6SB. I tested it at phone size: the steps change as you scroll and the text is readable without zooming.

How it works:
- The diagram stays fixed on screen. Each bit of scrolling moves the view to the next step and zooms in on it.
- The current step glows blue, along with the arrows leading into it. Steps you've passed stay solid, and the ones coming up are faded, so you always see how things link.
- Arrows are labeled with outcomes like yes, no, approve, skip, and "no, 3h+" (the Mac has been asleep over 3 hours).
- The top bar shows where the step runs (Google cloud, Telegram, You, Your Mac) and which step you're on, out of 16.
- The bottom card explains the current step in one or two lines.
- Step 1 shows the whole map. The last step zooms back out and lights up every Telegram box, so you can see at a glance where it's used.

Dashed borders mean a decision; solid borders mean an action.

# Flow doctor

You run Sejal's automations like a careful engineer explaining to a designer: find the broken step, fix it safely, prove it with a dry run, and explain the flow in plain words.

`<studio>` below means this plugin's root: two folders up from this skill's base directory. Read `<studio>/shared/house-rules.md` first (and `private.local.md` if present). Read `verify.md`, `truth.md` and `handoff.md` when the step below points to them.

## Hard limits (never cross, even if asked mid-task)

- No getting past CAPTCHAs or bot checks. If a site shows one, stop that source and mark it "Needs you".
- No passwords, tokens or cookies in code, files, commits or chat. She logs in herself in the script's own browser profile.
- Private answers (pay, phone, notice period) stay in git-ignored files and are never printed in full.
- Unknown form question: skip and mark "Needs you", never guess.
- Respect daily caps per site. No paid tools or APIs without her OK.
- Messages to people (recruiters, cold emails, DMs) are drafted only; she sends them.
- Warn her plainly when a step breaks a site's terms (for example LinkedIn automation) and record her decision in the tracker before building it.

## 1. Intake

- Run `bash <studio>/scripts/detect.sh`. The repo README is the tracker: read its Requirements, Answers, What exists and Status sections before anything else.
- Turn her note into a numbered checklist ordered by priority. Tag items `flow`, `code`, `copy`. Site-side changes (resume link, `?ref=` tracking, analytics) go to `studio:code-doctor` through `handoff.md`.

## 2. Reproduce

- Find the failing run: Apps Script execution log, local script output, or the Sheet row with Status "Needs you" and its reason.
- Rerun the failing step in dry-run (no writes, no applies) and capture the exact error or the selector that no longer matches.

## 3. Diagnose

For each item, one line: **where** (script, function, step), **why** it breaks (site changed, selector moved, quota, login expired, duplicate, timing), **fix**.

Explain the flow when it matters, as nodes and edges in plain words:
`Trigger (every hour) → Fetch new posts → Filter (role, level, freshness) → Already logged? → Apply or mark Needs you → Log row`.
For a bigger change, offer a small HTML flow diagram she can scroll through.

## 4. Fix

- Smallest change that removes the cause. Selectors by role and visible text before brittle CSS paths.
- Every step idempotent: rerunning never applies twice or adds duplicate rows.
- Log every action with a reason, so the Sheet explains itself.
- Cover notes and answers that describe her work go through `truth.md`.

## 5. Verify

Follow `verify.md` (automation section): dry run with log lines as proof, then one real item only after her OK, then show the resulting row. Report counts: found, skipped (with reasons), done, needs her.

## 6. Report

- Update the README Status (done, decided, next, left).
- Reply in about 5 lines in plain language: what broke, what changed, what was proven, what she must do (a login, a decision), the next source or step.

# Studio

One command for every chat. You read the room, plan, route each item to the right doctor, and hand back one report with proof.

`<studio>` below means this plugin's root: two folders up from this skill's base directory.

**Studio board:** if the `mcp__studio-board__progress` tool is available, keep the side panel current: project and chat after reading the room, the checklist once planned, each item's status as it changes, each phase change, and what needs her. Send only what changed.

## 1. Read the room

Run `bash <studio>/scripts/detect.sh "<her message>"` and combine its output with the message:

| Signal | Route |
|---|---|
| `kind=code` (web app repo), or the message names pages, components, bugs on the site, deploys | `studio:code-doctor` |
| Figma link or node id in the message, or the message is about frames, prototypes, style tiles, resume layout | `studio:figma-doctor` |
| `kind=automation`, or the message is about scripts, the Sheet, applying, job sources | `studio:flow-doctor` |
| Mixed | Split by item (step 2) |
| No signal | Ask one question: which project and where it lives |

Also read the tracker it found (TIMELINE.md or the README status). Its "Chats" section says which chat owns what. If an item belongs to a different chat (for example cat art, or Figma work while you are in the code chat), it is handed off, not done here.

## 2. Plan

Turn her note into one numbered checklist:

```
P0  [code]   Hero subtext unreadable in dark mode
P1  [figma]  Case study 02 top section: spacing
P2  [copy]   Resume line about the quiz
P3  [cat]    → cat chat (hand-off)
```

- Keep her order when she gave one; otherwise P0 = broken or embarrassing, then what a recruiter sees first, then polish.
- When she gives a token budget or says "conserve", mark each item S / M / L and cut to what fits. Deferred items go to the tracker, not into silence.
- Map dependencies: design decisions before the code that implements them, Figma before code when Figma is the source, copy before layout when length matters.
- Ambiguous items: ask now, numbered, in one message. Do not start them until answered.
- For a big round, show the plan first and wait for her go. For a short list of clear fixes, start.

## 3. Route

- Load the doctor for the items in this chat and follow its steps. It loads the shared rules, the taste gate and the truth check as needed.
- Items for another chat: send them through `<studio>/shared/handoff.md` and note them in the tracker.
- Independent items can run in parallel subagents. Use a cheaper model for read-only work (audits, research, screenshots), keep design and fixing in this session, and never let two helpers write the same files.
- A doctor can call another mid-item (for example code-doctor calling figma-doctor to update the frame it just matched). Finish the hand-off, then return to the checklist.

## 4. Close the loop

Before reporting:

- Every item has proof per `<studio>/shared/verify.md`, or an honest "not verified: why, and how she can check".
- `python3 <studio>/scripts/copy-lint.py --diff` is clean, or each hit is explained.
- The tracker is updated: done, open, deferred, hand-offs, decisions.

## 5. Report

One message, about 5 lines plus the proof files:

- Per item: done / partly / blocked, in a few words.
- What is verified and what is not.
- Preview link or Figma node, if any.
- The one decision or action she needs to take next.

Send screenshots with SendUserFile. Never merge or ship to production without her explicit OK.

## ASSISTANT
First, quick answers to the questions you asked last time:
- **How the script works:** it's a Node.js program that runs on your Mac. It opens a real Chrome window and clicks and types like a person would, using a free tool called Playwright. Nothing is injected into Instahyre's website.
- **Moving to another laptop:** the code is in your private GitHub repo, https://github.com/SBorkar2002/job-hunt-automation. Two small private files (your settings file and your answers) never go to GitHub, so you copy those across by hand, then log in to Instahyre again on the new laptop. The steps are in the README.
- **Links:** the Sheet and Apps Script links will exist after steps 1 and 2 below. I'll collect all of them for you at the end.

## Step 1 of 7: create the Apps Script project

I've already copied the code to your Mac's clipboard. If you're doing this on another device, tell me and I'll give you another way to get the code.

1. Sign in to your **personal** Google account in Chrome, not Epifi.
2. Go to **script.google.com** and click **New project** (top left).
3. Click "Untitled project" at the top and rename it **Job hunt bot**.
4. In the editor, select all the sample code (`function myFunction() {...}`) and delete it.
5. Paste with **Cmd + V**. You should see about 187 lines, starting with `/** Job hunt tracker: shared setup.`
6. Press **Cmd + S** to save.

Reply **"done 1"** and I'll give you step 2 (creating the Sheet). If anything looks different from what I describe, send me a screenshot.

If that pastes something else, copy everything in the box below instead. On most screens there's a copy button in the box's top right corner.

```javascript
/**
 * Job hunt tracker: shared setup.
 * Runs inside Google Apps Script on Sejal's personal Google account,
 * so the Sheet side works even when her laptop is off.
 *
 * Secrets (sheet id, SHEET_SECRET) live in Script Properties,
 * never in this file.
 */

const TABS = ['LinkedIn', 'Instahyre', 'Wellfound', 'Cutshort', 'Career pages'];

const COLUMNS = [
  'Found at', 'Posted at', 'Applied at', 'Minutes to apply',
  'Company', 'Role', 'Location / mode', 'Applicants',
  'Job link', 'Post link', 'Recruiter / poster',
  'Resume version', 'Note used', 'Action', 'Status',
  'Response at', 'Follow-up on', 'Notes',
];

const STATUSES = ['Applied', 'Needs you', 'Viewed', 'Replied', 'Interview', 'Offer', 'Rejected', 'No reply', 'Skipped'];
const ACTIONS = ['Applied', 'Needs you', 'Skipped'];

const props = () => PropertiesService.getScriptProperties();

/** Step 1. Run once. Creates the sheet with one tab per source. */
function setupTracker() {
  let id = props().getProperty('SHEET_ID');
  const ss = id ? SpreadsheetApp.openById(id) : SpreadsheetApp.create('Job hunt tracker');
  if (!id) props().setProperty('SHEET_ID', ss.getId());
  ss.setSpreadsheetTimeZone('Asia/Kolkata');

  TABS.forEach(name => setupTab_(ss, name));

  const blank = ss.getSheetByName('Sheet1');
  if (blank && ss.getSheets().length > 1) ss.deleteSheet(blank);

  Logger.log('Tracker ready: ' + ss.getUrl());
}

/** Adds a new source tab later, for example addTab('Naukri'). */
function addTab(name) {
  setupTab_(sheet_(), name);
}

function setupTab_(ss, name) {
  const sh = ss.getSheetByName(name) || ss.insertSheet(name);
  sh.getRange(1, 1, 1, COLUMNS.length).setValues([COLUMNS]).setFontWeight('bold');
  sh.setFrozenRows(1);

  const col = n => COLUMNS.indexOf(n) + 1;
  const rows = sh.getMaxRows() - 1;
  const list = values => SpreadsheetApp.newDataValidation().requireValueInList(values, true).build();
  sh.getRange(2, col('Status'), rows).setDataValidation(list(STATUSES));
  sh.getRange(2, col('Action'), rows).setDataValidation(list(ACTIONS));
  ['Found at', 'Posted at', 'Applied at', 'Response at'].forEach(n =>
    sh.getRange(2, col(n), rows).setNumberFormat('dd mmm yyyy, hh:mm'));
  sh.getRange(2, col('Follow-up on'), rows).setNumberFormat('dd mmm yyyy');
}

function sheet_() {
  const id = props().getProperty('SHEET_ID');
  if (!id) throw new Error('Run setupTracker first.');
  return SpreadsheetApp.openById(id);
}

/**
 * Logs one job to a source tab. Skips it if the job link is already there.
 * job: { company, role, location, applicants, jobLink, postLink, poster, postedAt, appliedAt,
 *        resume, note, action, status, notes }
 * Returns true when a new row was added.
 */
function addJob(tab, job) {
  const sh = sheet_().getSheetByName(tab);
  const linkCol = COLUMNS.indexOf('Job link') + 1;
  const last = sh.getLastRow();
  if (last > 1 && job.jobLink) {
    const links = sh.getRange(2, linkCol, last - 1).getValues().flat();
    if (links.includes(job.jobLink)) return false;
  }

  const row = {
    'Found at': new Date(),
    'Posted at': job.postedAt || '',
    'Company': job.company || '',
    'Role': job.role || '',
    'Location / mode': job.location || '',
    'Applicants': job.applicants || '',
    'Job link': job.jobLink || '',
    'Post link': job.postLink || '',
    'Recruiter / poster': job.poster || '',
    'Applied at': job.appliedAt || '',
    'Resume version': job.resume || '',
    'Note used': job.note || '',
    'Action': job.action || 'Applied',
    'Status': job.status || job.action || 'Applied',
    'Notes': job.notes || '',
  };
  sh.appendRow(COLUMNS.map(c => (c in row ? row[c] : '')));

  // Minutes to apply fills itself once Applied at is set.
  const r = sh.getLastRow();
  sh.getRange(r, COLUMNS.indexOf('Minutes to apply') + 1)
    .setFormulaR1C1('=IF(OR(R[0]C3="",R[0]C2=""),"",ROUND((R[0]C3-R[0]C2)*1440))');
  return true;
}

/* ---------- Endpoint for the site scripts on the Mac ---------- */

/**
 * The Mac scripts post here to log rows. Deploy once as a web app
 * (Deploy > New deployment > Web app, execute as Me, access Anyone).
 * Requests must carry SHEET_SECRET from Script Properties, so nobody
 * else can write to the Sheet.
 *
 * Body: { secret, action: 'has' | 'add', tab, job }
 */
function doPost(e) {
  const out = obj => ContentService.createTextOutput(JSON.stringify(obj))
    .setMimeType(ContentService.MimeType.JSON);
  try {
    const body = JSON.parse(e.postData.contents);
    if (!body.secret || body.secret !== props().getProperty('SHEET_SECRET')) return out({ ok: false, error: 'bad secret' });
    if (!TABS.includes(body.tab) && !sheet_().getSheetByName(body.tab)) return out({ ok: false, error: 'unknown tab' });

    if (body.action === 'has') return out({ ok: true, has: hasJob_(body.tab, body.job.jobLink) });
    if (body.action === 'add') return out({ ok: true, added: addJob(body.tab, body.job) });
    return out({ ok: false, error: 'unknown action' });
  } catch (err) {
    return out({ ok: false, error: String(err) });
  }
}

function hasJob_(tab, link) {
  const sh = sheet_().getSheetByName(tab);
  const last = sh.getLastRow();
  if (last < 2 || !link) return false;
  return sh.getRange(2, COLUMNS.indexOf('Job link') + 1, last - 1).getValues().flat().includes(link);
}

/** Run once to create the secret the Mac scripts use. Copy it from the log into the local .env file. */
function makeSecret() {
  const s = Utilities.getUuid().replace(/-/g, '');
  props().setProperty('SHEET_SECRET', s);
  Logger.log('SHEET_SECRET=' + s);
}
```

After pasting, you should see 145 lines. Press **Cmd + S** to save, then reply **"done 1"** and I'll give you step 2.

## Step 4 of 7: turn the script into a web app

This gives your Mac scripts an address they can send Sheet rows to.

1. In Apps Script, click the blue **Deploy** button (top right), then **New deployment**.

3. Fill in:
   - **Description:** Job hunt logger
   - **Execute as:** Me
   - **Who has access:** Anyone. This is needed so your Mac can reach it without a Google login. The secret still blocks everyone else.
4. Click **Deploy**. If it asks for permissions again, allow them the same way as before.
5. Copy the **Web app URL**. It ends in `/exec`.

## Step 7 of 7: log in to Instahyre

A new Chrome window should have opened on the Instahyre login page. It's the bot's own Chrome, separate from your normal one.

1. Log in there as you normally do. If Instahyre sends an OTP, enter it yourself.

## ASSISTANT
Nothing is broken now. Here's what happened:

1. **First try:** the bot found **Apply now**, but on Instahyre's page that button sits inside a link that catches the mouse. The bot's click kept landing on the link and timed out after about 30 seconds. **No application was sent** and nothing was logged.
2. **The fix:** I checked the page (read-only) and found the real button, the one that runs Instahyre's own apply action. The bot now presses that button directly.
3. **Now:** the retry for Foresight Labs is running in the bot's Chrome window. Please don't close it. When it finishes I'll show you what happened and the Sheet row.

2D Artist
OpEzee
Bangalore2-4 Years
Sanjana S
Recruiter at Multi Recruit
 Job Description

Function: Design & Creative → Graphic Design / Animation, UX / Visual Design, Marketing → Advertising / Creative

* Adobe Illustrator
* Adobe Photoshop
* Adobe Premiere Pro
* After Effects
* +6 more
* Illustrator
* Photoshop
* Premiere Pro
* animation
* graphic design
* motion graphics


We are seeking a skilled 2D Artist with a strong background in graphic design and motion graphics to create captivating visual content for various projects. The ideal candidate will have a keen eye for detail, a creative mindset, and the ability to translate ideas into visually compelling graphics and animations.

Responsibilities:

* Design and create high-quality 2D graphics for various media, including digital and print.
* Develop motion graphics and animations for video content, marketing materials, and social media campaigns.
* Collaborate with the creative team to conceptualize and execute visually appealing designs that align with project goals.
* Edit and refine graphics and animations based on feedback and project requirements.
* Ensure consistency in visual style and branding across all projects.
* Stay updated with the latest design trends, tools, and technologies to continuously improve skills and deliver innovative solutions.
* Manage multiple projects simultaneously and meet deadlines in a fast-paced environment.
* Work closely with other team members, including writers, directors, and producers, to ensure seamless integration of visual elements into overall project execution.


Requirements:

* Bachelor's degree in graphic design, animation, fine arts, or a related field.
* Proven experience as a 2D artist, graphic designer, or motion graphics designer.
* Proficiency in graphic design software such as Adobe Creative Suite (Photoshop, Illustrator, After Effects, and Premiere Pro).
* Strong portfolio showcasing a range of graphic design and motion graphics projects.
* Excellent understanding of design principles, typography, color theory, and animation techniques.
* Ability to work independently and collaboratively in a team environment.
* Strong communication skills and the ability to effectively present and articulate design concepts.
* Attention to detail and a commitment to producing high-quality work.
* Time management skills and the ability to prioritize tasks and meet deadlines.


Preferred Skills:

* Experience with 2D software (e. g., Photoshop, After Effects, and Premiere Pro).
* Knowledge of 3D design and animation tools (e. g., Blender, Cinema 4D) is a plus.
* Experience in video editing and post-production.
* Familiarity with user interface (UI) and user experience (UX) design principles.


 About OpEzee

OpEzee brings smart, innovative solutions for the infrastructure sector in India. We focus on Smart Highways, Smart Cities, Smart Campuses and Smart Buildings.

OpEzee products includes SIOT Solutions, 3D Holographic Solutions, and Tech Marketplace.

SIOT Solutions - Smart IoT Solutions for Smart Cities/Smart Campuses.

OpEzee Marketplace - India's first structured marketplace for electronic equipment, sensors, services procurement.

Built with experience in procurement and equipment/service management for highways and smart cities - the services provides the ability to consolidate your electronic equipment/sensors needs and procure effectively through an online platform. We provide quote options, best rates, services and preferred vendor solutions to meet your business needs.

Our consultative approach helps you find the right solution fitment to adapt to your futuristic needs. Our client base includes Real Estate, Smart City Service Providers, Concessionaires etc.

 OpEzee's Tech Stack

* IoT

 Why Explore a Career at OpEzee

* Looking for a very interesting change in your career.
* Working under challenging conditions
* Chance to constantly improve
* Work with cutting-edge technology

isn't this a fitting role?

also - does the automation script that we wrote - is it for instahyre only?

## ASSISTANT
No, I'd say this one isn't a fit, and the bot skipped it on purpose. Its log line was: `skip: OpEzee | 2D Artist (title "2D Artist" not in your list)`.

Why it doesn't fit:
- **The title isn't one of yours.** The bot only applies to Product Designer, UX Designer, UI/UX Designer, Interaction Designer, and AI/GenAI Product Designer. That's the list you approved.
- **The work is different.** This role is graphic design and motion graphics: After Effects, Premiere Pro, video editing, print. UX only shows up as a "nice to have".
- **Experience is 2 to 4 years.** That's borderline for your 0 to 2 range, but it passes, since the minimum is 2.

My view: if the bot sends 2D artist and graphic design applications under your name, recruiters may see you as a graphic designer rather than a product designer. That can weaken the product design story your portfolio tells.

That said, you do have real art skills (comics, pixel art), so if you also want art roles, that's a fair choice. Your options:
1. **Keep it as is** (my pick): product and UX roles only.
2. **Add "Visual Designer" and "UI Designer"**: close to product work, and they'd catch roles like Zepto and Plaza.
3. **Add art roles too**: 2D Artist, Graphic Designer, Brand Designer, Illustrator. I'd use a different cover note for these, since the current one talks about product design.

Tell me 1, 2 or 3 and I'll update the list.

**Is the script only for Instahyre?** The applier part is: `instahyre/apply.mjs` knows Instahyre's pages and buttons. The shared parts get reused for every site: your filters and cover note (`shared/config.json`), your saved answers, the Sheet logging, and the hourly timer. So Cutshort will be a new `cutshort/apply.mjs` that plugs into the same pieces and writes to the Cutshort tab of the same Sheet.

No, you don't need to touch the Apps Script. The last change to its code was removing Telegram, and that was before you pasted it, so your copy is already the latest.

Here's why. The Apps Script only does three things: it creates the Sheet tabs, checks whether a job is already logged, and adds rows. Deciding which jobs fit (titles, pay, notes) happens in the scripts on your Mac, and I update those myself.

If the Apps Script ever does need a change, like a new column or a new tab, I'll tell you. Since it lives in your Google account and I can't edit it, you'd paste the new code and redeploy, and I'd walk you through it step by step.

## ASSISTANT
PR #3 is merged ([SBorkar2002/job-hunt-automation#3](https://github.com/SBorkar2002/job-hunt-automation/pull/3)) and the timer is refreshed. I can't open your Sheet, so I haven't seen a row in the new format yet; the first one appears with the next application.

**Run result:** 8 found, **2 applied** (OpEzee 2D Artist, Plaza Visual Designer), 1 Needs you (IDEX Graphic Designer, no pay shown), and 5 skipped because they're in Mumbai or Gurgaon. That makes 3 applied today, out of 10.

**You were right about the columns.** "site-v1" and "note-v1" were my internal codes, and worse, they were misleading:
- **Resume version** said "site-v1", which suggested your portfolio PDF went out. Instahyre's one-click apply actually sends the resume on your **Instahyre profile**. Now it says exactly that.
- **Note used** said "note-v1", which suggested a note was sent. None of these applications asked for one. Now it shows the exact note text when one is sent, or "None (one-click apply, no note asked)".
- **Applied at** was written in UTC as plain text. Now it's IST and the Sheet reads it as a real date.
- **Recruiter / poster** is now filled from the job page, for example "Sanjana S, Recruiter at Multi Recruit".
- **Notes** now shows the role type (product, visual, art, brand or graphic), the experience asked, and pay if shown.

Columns that stay blank for Instahyre, because Instahyre doesn't show them: **Posted at, Applicants, Post link**, and therefore **Minutes to apply**. They'll fill in for sites that do show them.

**Your 3 older rows** (Foresight Labs, OpEzee, Plaza) still have the old codes. If you want, edit them by hand: set Resume to "Instahyre profile resume" and Note to "None (one-click apply, no note asked)".

## ASSISTANT
The first round is built and tested in a dry run: [SBorkar2002/job-hunt-automation#5](https://github.com/SBorkar2002/job-hunt-automation/pull/5). It's not merged yet, and the hourly timer still runs the old code until it is.

**What's done:**
- **Shared runner.** Instahyre now plugs into one shared engine through its five parts (login, find jobs, read a job, apply, check if applied). Wellfound will plug into the same engine, so filters, the Sheet, caps, pacing and the "Needs you" recheck work identically everywhere.
- **Start-of-run check.** If a site's page changes, the bot stops that site and saves a capture instead of clicking around. It keeps only the newest 12 captures.
- **Same job on two sites.** It applies once. It applies again on a second site only if there's been no reply after 7 days. Your 4 applications so far are remembered.
- **`npm run status`** shows each site's health on your Mac.
- **Sheet (written, not live yet):** a red/green **Health** tab, a **Runs** tab with one row per run, and an automatic fix for your 3 old rows.

**Proof:** a dry run on your live Instahyre feed found 5 jobs, skipped all 5 for location (Mumbai, Gurgaon), and reported health OK.

**To put the Sheet changes live without you pasting code again, there's a one-time setup (about 2 minutes):**
1. Go to **script.google.com/home/usersettings**, signed in with your personal Google account, and turn **Google Apps Script API** to **On**.
2. In this folder, run this command:

```bash
npx @google/clasp login
```

   It opens a browser window. Pick your **personal** Google account and click **Allow**.

Reply **"clasp done"** and I'll deploy the Sheet side myself, check the Health and Runs tabs, then ask you before merging PR #5 and switching the timer over.

## ASSISTANT
That error wasn't your fault. Your Mac's npm download folder (`~/.npm`) has a file owned by the admin account, probably left over from an old `sudo npm` command. That blocked npm from saving clasp there.

I worked around it by installing clasp inside this project instead. Run this in the [terminal](#terminal/0):

```bash
npm run clasp:login
```

A browser tab opens. Pick your **personal** Google account and click **Allow**. If you see "Google hasn't verified this app", click **Continue**. The terminal should then say you're logged in.

Make sure **Apps Script API** is **On** at script.google.com/home/usersettings too. Then reply **"clasp done"**.

Optional, for later: to fix the npm folder for good, run `sudo chown -R $(whoami) ~/.npm` yourself. It needs your Mac password, so I won't run it.

## ASSISTANT
That explains it. Google blocks sign-in inside any browser a script controls, as protection against bots. So "Continue with Google" couldn't work in the bot's window.

The fix: login now opens a **normal** Chrome window that still uses the bot's profile. You sign in yourself there, and the bot reuses that login later. The bot itself never signs in.

**Next:**
1. A new Chrome window should be open on Wellfound's login page. It's separate from your usual Chrome.
2. Click **Continue with Google** and pick your **personal** account.
3. When you see your Wellfound jobs or profile, **quit that Chrome with Cmd + Q**. Make sure it's that window, not your usual Chrome.
4. Reply **"done"**.

The script then checks the login worked and tells me.

## ASSISTANT
Found the real cause. Wellfound's login cookie is the kind Chrome deletes whenever it restarts. The "On startup" setting didn't save it, because the bot's way of starting Chrome skips that.

The new login step fixes it. Once you're logged in, the script asks that same Chrome to keep Wellfound's login for 30 days. It stays inside the bot's Chrome profile, and nothing is copied anywhere. After 30 days, the Health tab will say "Login expired" and you log in again.

**One last time:**
1. A Chrome window just opened on Wellfound's login page.
2. **Continue with Google** and pick your personal account.
3. **Don't close anything.** The window closes by itself a few seconds after you're in.
4. Reply **"done"**.

The script then restarts Chrome and checks you're still logged in.

Sorry for the back and forth. Last time, the script closed Chrome too early, in the middle of your Google sign-in. Now **you** decide when it's done:

1. A Chrome window just opened on Wellfound's login page.
2. **Continue with Google** and pick your personal account. If Wellfound asks onboarding questions, finish them.
3. Wait until you see **your name or photo** on Wellfound (top right).
4. Then **quit that Chrome with Cmd + Q**.
5. Reply **"done"**.

While the window is open, the script quietly keeps Wellfound's login cookie from being deleted. After you quit, it restarts Chrome and checks you're still logged in.

## ASSISTANT
I found the real cause, and it wasn't anything you did. The bot's Chrome locks cookies with a stand-in key, while the login Chrome locks them with your Mac's real keychain. So your login was saved, but the bot couldn't read it. For Wellfound, the bot now uses the real keychain too.

**Please log in once more:**
1. A Chrome window is open on Wellfound. **Continue with Google**, and finish any onboarding.
2. Once you see your **name or photo**, quit that Chrome with **Cmd + Q**.
3. **If your Mac asks** whether Chrome can use "Chrome Safe Storage", click **Always Allow**. That's Chrome's own keychain entry, the one it uses to lock cookies.
4. Reply **"done"**.

The script then restarts the bot's Chrome and checks the login stuck.

## ASSISTANT
Naukri isn't built yet. Right now it's on the "later" list, behind Cutshort. It can be next if you want. Here's my take before you decide:

**For Naukri:** it has the most jobs of any Indian job site, recruiters actively search it, and many companies post there before anywhere else.

**Against Naukri, for your roles:**
- Design jobs there lean toward agencies and IT services firms, which are on your "avoid" list. Cutshort and Wellfound have more product and startup roles.
- Many Naukri applications ask extra screening questions. The bot won't guess answers, so a good share would become "Needs you" rows for you to finish.
- Naukri is known for strict bot checks. If it shows a CAPTCHA, the bot stops and the Health tab turns red, so some days it may apply to nothing.

**My suggestion:** do Cutshort next as planned, then Naukri. If you'd rather have Naukri's volume first, I'll start it with the same steps: terms and your OK, profile check, a read-only recon, a filter check with you on 10 real jobs, one live application, then the timer. It would start at about 5 a day.

## ASSISTANT
Cutshort is skipped and recorded in the README. I've read Naukri's terms, and they're as strict as Cutshort's, plus a legal warning:

- **Bans:** using "automated means to crawl and/or scrape content", and extracting data "using any automated process such as spiders, crawlers".
- **Detection:** Naukri says it uses "automated systems and manual checks to detect unusual activity" and can limit or block accounts.
- **Worst case:** your Naukri account gets limited or blocked. The terms also say anyone found crawling or misusing its data is "liable to appropriate action in a court of law both civil and criminal". That's aimed mainly at data scrapers and resellers, not someone applying to jobs, but it's there.

My view: it's riskier than Wellfound, and the account is one recruiters actively search. Losing it hurts more than losing a smaller site. If you still want it, I'd start at **3 a day**, slow pacing, and stop at the first bot check.

A safer alternative: the bot only *finds* fresh Naukri design jobs and lists them in the Sheet as "Needs you", and you click Apply yourself. Naukri still sees a bot reading its pages, just much less activity.

Which do you want?
1. **Full auto-apply** at 3 a day, accepting the account risk.
2. **Find only**: the bot lists jobs, you apply.
3. **Skip Naukri** for now.

- under about 1.5 years in total: **0-1**

- about 3 to 4 years: **3-4**

what about glassdoor?

## ASSISTANT
My take: Glassdoor isn't worth automating for you.

- **In India, it's mostly a copy of other sites.** Most of its job listings come from LinkedIn, Naukri and company career pages. Applying usually sends you off to those same places, where the bot is either skipped (LinkedIn) or on hold (Naukri).
- **Few one-click applies there.** Most "Apply" buttons lead to company forms like the auxi one, which differ every time.
- **Terms:** Glassdoor, like the others, bans bots and scraping. I'd read its exact wording before building anything, but I don't expect anything different.

**Where it's useful:** company research. Before an interview, or when an offer comes, check the company's reviews and the salaries people report for designers there. That's a few minutes by hand, no bot needed.

**Two things are still open from earlier:**
1. **Naukri:** full auto-apply at 3 a day, find-only (the bot lists jobs, you apply), or skip?
2. **Your experience:** roughly when did your freelance work start? That decides 0-1 vs 2-3 years on forms like auxi's, and in the bot's experience filter.

## ASSISTANT
I read Glassdoor's terms. They ban using "software or automated agents" to "scrape, strip, or mine data" from the site. Glassdoor is also owned by Indeed, and the terms say losing access on one can cost you the other.

So even "find only" counts as scraping. But there's a route that **breaks no rules and works with your laptop off**: Glassdoor's own **job alert emails**.

1. **You set up one or two job alerts on Glassdoor** (for example "Product Designer, Bengaluru" and "UX Designer, Remote India"), sent to your personal Gmail. About 2 minutes, and I'll walk you through it.
2. **Your Apps Script reads those alert emails** in Gmail once an hour. Google runs it, so your Mac doesn't need to be on, and the bot never visits Glassdoor at all.
3. **Each new job goes into a "Glassdoor" tab** in your Sheet as "Needs you", with the link, after the same filters (title, level, city, pay if shown) and the same duplicate check against your other tabs.
4. **You click through and apply.** Many end up on company forms like auxi's anyway.

The only catch: Glassdoor sends alerts once a day, not instantly. For a find-only list, that's fine.

This needs one new permission when I deploy: the Apps Script reading your Gmail, limited to messages from Glassdoor.

Which do you want?
1. **Email alerts route** (my recommendation)
2. **Scrape Glassdoor directly** (against its terms, risks your Glassdoor and Indeed accounts)
3. **Skip Glassdoor**

Naukri and your freelance start date are also still open from before.

what else do you think we can automate? i dont have you for long suggest me everything

## ASSISTANT
Here's everything I'd automate, ranked by value for the time we have. All of it stays within the sites' rules and your hard limits. Nothing gets sent to people without you.

**Top priority** (biggest win, runs in Google's cloud, works with your laptop off):
1. **Gmail job-alert reader.** Reads the alert emails you already get, or can set up, from Glassdoor, LinkedIn, Naukri, Indeed and others. The fresh jobs go into the Sheet, through your filters and the duplicate check, as "Needs you". This covers Glassdoor, LinkedIn and Naukri with zero account risk. *About 1 session.*
2. **Reply tracker.** The same script spots recruiter replies, interview invites and rejections in Gmail and updates that job's Status automatically. You'd see your real reply rate without lifting a finger. *Half a session, built together with #1.*
3. **Follow-up reminders.** Any job Applied over 7 days ago with no reply gets a follow-up date and a drafted nudge message in the Sheet. You send it yourself. *Small.*
4. **Health alert and weekly summary by email.** If a site's Health stays red for over 3 hours, you get an email (from you, to you). Every Sunday: applied per site, replies, interviews, open "Needs you" rows. *Small.*

**Strong next:**

5. **Company career-page watcher.** You list 20 to 40 dream companies. The bot checks their public job boards (Greenhouse, Lever, Ashby, Rippling, which are meant to be read) every hour and catches roles before they reach LinkedIn. *About 1 session.*
6. **"Fill this form" helper.** For one-off company forms like auxi's. You paste a link, it opens a window with your answers filled in and drafts the note, and **you** check it and click Apply. *About 1 session.*
7. **Portfolio visit tracking.** `?ref=` tags plus free analytics on your site, so you can see which applications made someone open your portfolio. That's a site change, so it goes to your code chat. *Small.*

**Nice to have:**

8. **Interview prep pack.** When a Status becomes Interview, a doc is made with the job description, company notes, your best-matching case study and likely questions.
9. **Calendar.** Interview emails become calendar events, with your OK on each.

**So it keeps working after I'm gone:**

10. **A "Fix it yourself" runbook** in the README, with a `npm run doctor` command that checks every login and timer and prints the one command to fix each problem (for example "Wellfound logged out: run `npm run wellfound:login`").

**My suggested order:** 10 (short, protects everything else), then 1 and 2 together, then 3 and 4, then 5. That's roughly three focused sessions.

Still open: Glassdoor via email alerts (fully covered by #1), Naukri (#1 also covers it safely, which I'd now recommend over auto-apply), and your freelance start date.

Tell me which numbers to build, or say "go in your order".

- Allow the permissions. It asks to read your Gmail (to find alerts and replies) and to send email (only to you: health alerts and the Sunday summary). If you see "Google hasn't verified this app", click **Advanced**, then **Go to Job hunt bot**.
- The log should end with "Permissions granted. Timers: ...". That starts the hourly and daily timers.

**2. Turn on job alerts to your personal Gmail** (about 5 minutes, in your normal browser)
- **Glassdoor:** search "Product Designer", location Bengaluru. Click **Create job alert** and set it to daily. Do the same for "UX Designer, Remote".
- **Naukri:** search "Product Designer", Bengaluru, 0-2 years. Click **Create job alert**.
- **LinkedIn:** search "Product Designer", Bengaluru, past 24 hours. Turn on **Set alert**.
- **Indeed** (optional): the same search, then **Get new jobs by email**.

Reply **"done"** after step 1, even if the alerts aren't set up yet. I'll then run a test that reads your existing alert emails (read-only), check the bot picks out title, company and link correctly, and show you the result.

**What runs after that:**
- **Every hour:** new jobs from alert emails go into the right tab as "Needs you". Replies from companies update that row's Status (Replied, Interview or Rejected).
- **Daily at 9am:** jobs applied 7+ days ago with no reply get a drafted follow-up in a new **Follow-ups** tab. You send those yourself.
- **If a site is red for 3+ hours:** one email to you.
- **Sundays at 7pm:** your weekly summary email.

## ASSISTANT
All 3 are built ([SBorkar2002/job-hunt-automation#10](https://github.com/SBorkar2002/job-hunt-automation/pull/10), not merged yet).

1. **`npm run doctor`, plus a "Fix it yourself" table** at the top of the README. It checks logins, timers, the Sheet, clasp and pending rows, and prints the exact fix command for anything wrong. Today it reports all good.
2. **Career-page watcher.** Every hour in Google's cloud, it reads the public job boards of **CRED, Meesho, Zeta, Razorpay, Groww, InMobi, Figma, Atlys and Sarvam AI**. Matching roles land in the **Career pages** tab as "Needs you". Tell me any other companies and I'll add them.
3. **`npm run fill -- <job link>`.** It opens a company's form, attaches your resume, fills your details, drafts the note, and lists what's left. **You** check it and click Submit, and it logs the row once a thank-you page shows. ## ASSISTANT
My honest read: **the company is real, but this posting and the "CEO" look suspicious.** Treat it as a possible scam until it's verified.

**Real:** Recall Network exists. It's an AI and Web3 startup, reportedly backed by Multicoin Capital and Coinbase Ventures, with about $42M raised.

**Red flags:**
1. **The founder name doesn't match.** Public sources list Recall's CEO as **Andrew Hill**, with co-founders **Michael Sena** and **Sander Pick**. This Wellfound page lists one founder, **"Thomas Hansen"**, who doesn't appear in any source I found.
2. **It's a second company page.** Its address is `recall-network-2`. Scammers often make a copy of a real company's page.
3. **They can't hire you.** The post hires only in **Canada, the US and the UK**, and you're in India. A real CEO usually wouldn't message someone the job is closed to.
4. **It fits a known scam pattern.** Fake Web3 companies have bought ads on Wellfound and LinkedIn to target job seekers. They move you to Telegram, then ask you to install a "meeting app" or "test tool" that steals passwords and crypto wallets.

**Until it's verified, don't:**
- install any app or file they send,
- move the chat to Telegram or WhatsApp,
- connect a crypto wallet,
- share ID documents or bank details.

**To check it in 5 minutes:**
- Look for this job on **recall.network**, the official site (find its careers or jobs page).
- Search LinkedIn for "Thomas Hansen Recall". A real founder has a long-standing profile linked to the company.
- Or message Recall through its official site or X account and ask if this posting is theirs.

**Are you a match?** On skills, yes. It asks for a junior to mid-level designer with strong Figma, AI curiosity and a personal portfolio, which is a good fit for you. On location, no: the listing excludes India.

If you paste the CEO's message here, without any links they sent, I'll tell you whether it reads like a real founder or a script.

Sources:
- [CoinLaunch: Recall Network](https://coinlaunch.space/projects/recall-network/)
- [IQ.wiki: Andrew Hill](https://iq.wiki/wiki/andrew-hill.md?lang=zh)
- [Decrypt: "Crazy Evil" fake Web3 firm drains job seekers' wallets](https://decrypt.co/308027?amp=1)
- [PhishFort: fake recruitment agencies](https://phishfort.com/list-of-fake-recruitment-agencies-web3-scams/)
- [1inch: fake recruiters on LinkedIn](https://1inch.com/blog/post/fake-1inch-recruiters)

## ASSISTANT
So far this message is harmless. It's a generic, polite opener, and real founders and scammers both write like this. Your reply was fine too. The risk comes at the **next step**, so watch what he sends back.

**Safe signs:**
- He stays on Wellfound, or moves to email from an **@recall.network** address.
- He suggests a normal Google Meet or Zoom link.
- He answers the location question clearly.

**Stop if:**
- He asks you to move to **Telegram or WhatsApp**.
- He sends a file, an app, a "test task" to download, or a meeting link that makes you install something.
- He asks you to connect a crypto wallet, or for ID or bank details before any real interview.
- He offers the job fast without a proper interview.

**A reply you could send next**, which checks him without being rude:

> Thanks, Thomas. Before we go further, could you share a bit more about the team and how the process works? I'm based in Bengaluru, and the listing says Canada, the US and the UK, so I wanted to check you're open to hiring in India. Happy to set up a call. Could you send an invite from your Recall Network email?

A real founder answers this easily. A scammer usually dodges the email request, or pushes you to Telegram or a download.

When he replies, paste it here and I'll read it with you.
